The position is a standard Petrov's Defense. White's most common and strong move here is Nf3xe5.

Let's analyze Nf3xe5:
If White plays 3. Nxe5, Black often responds with 3... d6 (to kick the knight) or 3... Nxe4.
If 3. Nxe5 d6 4. Nf3 Nxe4. This leads to a symmetric position.
If 3. Nxe5 Nxe4, then 4. Qa4+ (forking K and N).

Considering these, 3. Nxe5 is the main line.

Can I attack it? No.

  - Defends f1? No.

  - Blunder (Rxf2).

Same column. No check.

After `h4h3`, the material is White King vs Black King and Bishop.

Therefore, the best move is to capture the pawn.
